Developments relating to the Internal Market Informa-
tion System
As previously, the ZASt also organised a basic training 
course and a workshop for the German data protecti-
on supervisory authorities in relation to cross-border 
cooperation in the Internal Market Information System 
(IMI) in the current reporting year. Both events were 
very well received again. 
Cooperation with the Federal Ministry for Economic Af-
fairs and Climate Protection (BMWK), which represents 
Germany in general IMI matters vis-à-vis the European 
Commission, and with the national IMI Coordinator 
in the Federal Office of Administration (BV A) was also 
strengthened. The ZASt was able to provide the BMWK 
with important input from the practical work of the Ger-
man and European data protection supervisory authori-
ties for the meeting
of the European IMI Committee and for a report to the 
Federal Audit Office. In view of the large number of 
users in the IMI module for cooperation of data protec-
tion supervisory authorities and their institutionalised 
interaction via the IT Users Expert Subgroup of the 
EDPB, considerable user knowledge can be generated. 
As the central German interface and representative of 
the federal government on the IT Users Expert Subg-
roup, the ZASt always endeavours to make use of existing 
expertise to improve the IMI system. Cooperation in this 
context will therefore expand and intensify further.
Coordination of EU-funded projects
The coordination processes between the German su-
pervisory authorities were also further improved in a 
new development during the reporting year. Following 
preliminary conceptual work by the ZASt, the processes 
relating to applications for EU-funded projects by the 
federal and state data protection supervisory authorities 
were reorganised. In order to continue to respond quick-
ly and efficiently to funding bodies such as the European 
Commission, the role of coordinating applications by 
German supervisory authorities was transferred to the 
ZASt after appropriate consultation in the Organisation 
& Structure working group of the DSK. At the same time, 
the final decision on any applications for such projects 
remains solely with the data protection supervisory 
authorities themselves. Here too, the ZASt acts as an 
interface between the German supervisory authorities 
and the EU. This has not only expanded the coordination 
portfolio of the ZASt, but also takes into account the le-
gal provisions of Section 18 BDSG and GDPR Recital 119, 
according to which it is also responsible for controlling 
communication with the European Commission.
Address distribution list of specific supervisory autho-
rities
The cooperation of the DSK with the so-called specific 
supervisory authorities in the areas of broadcasting 
and religion, as required by the BDSG, was given new 
impetus. In order to strengthen the involvement of the 
specific supervisory authorities in the work of the DSK 
between its meetings and those of its working groups, 
the ZASt has set up and published an address list follo-
wing a resolution to this effect at the 104th DSK. This en-
ables the data protection supervisory authorities of the 
DSK and the specific supervisory authorities to identify 
the right contacts for effective interaction at any time. 
By publishing the address distribution list on the BfDI 
website with further explanations, the public also has 
access to this information. A corresponding link to this 
publication on the DSK website adds the finishing touch 
to this information service.
Consultation procedure for codes of conduct within 
Germany
The consultation processes between the German su-
pervisory authorities for company codes of conduct in 
accordance with Art. 40 GDPR have been defined. A sub-
group of the DSK’s Organisation and Structure working 
group, with the participation of the ZASt, has drawn up 
a proposal for an internal German procedure. This now 
ensures that a German data protection supervisory au-
thority takes the lead in dealing with the legal issues sur-
rounding codes of conduct. Continuous support for the 
process at both German and European level is therefore 
in place. The information processes and opportunities 
for participation that have now been regulated also gu-
arantee the chance for substantive participation on the 
part of all German supervisory authorities. The proposal 
for the German domestic procedure was adopted at the 
108th Data Protection Conference in November 2024.
